Appendix A
Proof of Theorem
1

In this section, we will show the proof of Theorem
1
. We first use standard perturbation analysis on the eigenspaces of
\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}

(
Vu et al. 2013
)
. By assumption,
\bm{\Pi}_{g}
and
\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}
are the projections onto top eigenspaces of
\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}
, therefore for any orthogonal projection matrix
\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}
and
\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)},\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left(\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)},\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle-\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}}\left(\left(\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right)\left\langle\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)},\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\lambda_{1}\left(\left(\bm{I}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right)\left\langle\bm{I}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\delta\left(r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle\right)

Summing both sides for
i
from 1 to
N
, we have:

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)},\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle\geq\delta\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle\right)

(32)

Since
\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}
and
\{\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\}
are the optimal solutions to (
7
), and
\bm{\Pi}_{g}
and
\{\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\}
are feasible, we know that:

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle\bm{S}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle\bm{S}_{(i)},\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle

(33)

Combining (
32
) and (
33
), we can obtain:

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle\geq\delta\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rangle\right)

We can use the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality to further bound the left-hand side as:

\left\langle\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}

Notice that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-2\left\langle\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}},\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\sqrt{r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}},\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle}

We thus have:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)},\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}},\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle\right]}

by another application of Cauchy-Schwartz inequality.

Finally:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}+\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}},\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle\right]\leq\frac{2}{N\delta^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

(34)

The relation (
34
) slightly extends the standard result from matrix perturbation theory. However, it only shows the summation of
\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}
and
\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}
is close to the summation of
\bm{\Pi}_{g}
and
\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}
. One cannot infer additional information about the closeness of
\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}
to
\bm{\Pi}_{g}
, or
\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}
to
\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}
. In other words, (
34
) alone does not ensure that the recovered global and local PCs correspond to true PCs.

Such a guarantee is too weak in practice when we want to know if the solved
\bm{U}
and
\bm{V}_{(i)}
’s are close to the ground truth. Fortunately, we can show that this is indeed the case if the problem satisfies the identifiability assumption
4.2
. An important finding is the following lemma, which indicates that the closeness in direct sum space can lead to closeness in each global and local subspaces.

Lemma 13
.

Suppose for
i=1,\cdots,N
,
\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}
,
\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}
and
\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}^{\star}
,
\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}^{\star}
are projection matrices satisfying
\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}=0
and
\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}^{\star}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}^{\star}=0
for each
i
. Among them,
\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}
and
\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}^{\star}
have rank
r_{1}
,
\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}
and
\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}^{\star}
have rank
r_{2,(i)}
. If there exists a positive constant
\theta>0
such that

\lambda_{max}(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}^{\star})\leq 1-\theta

We have the following bound:

\sum_{i=1}^{N}r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}+\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}},\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}^{\star}+\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}^{\star}\right\rangle\leq N\left(r_{1}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}^{\star},\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}\right\rangle\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{N}r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}^{\star},\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\right\rangle

(35)

Also:

\sum_{i=1}^{N}r_{1}+r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}+\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}},\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}^{\star}+\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}^{\star}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\theta}{2}\left(N\left(r_{1}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}^{\star},\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}\right\rangle\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{N}r_{2,(i)}-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}^{\star},\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\right\rangle\right)

(36)

The proof of Lemma
13
is at the end of Section
G
. By applying inequality (
36
) to (
34
), we can prove the desired error bound in Theorem
1
.

Appendix B
KKT condition

We show the KKT conditions (
24
). The lagrangian to the objective (
7
) is:

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left(\bm{U}^{T}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left(\bm{V}_{(i)}^{T}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i)}\right)+\left\langle\bm{\Lambda}_{2,(i)},\bm{V}_{(i)}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i)}-\bm{I}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bm{\Lambda}_{3,(i)},\bm{U}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i)}\right\rangle\right]

(37)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\bm{\Lambda}_{1},\bm{U}^{T}\bm{U}\right\rangle

where
\bm{\Lambda}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}}
,
\bm{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{2,(i)}\times r_{2,(i)}}
, and
\bm{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2,(i)}}
are dual variables. The KKT conditions are:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i)}+\bm{V}_{(i)}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}+\bm{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}^{T}\right)+\bm{U}\bm{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}=0\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}+\bm{V}_{(i)}\bm{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}^{T}\right]+\bm{U}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{1}+\bm{\Lambda}_{1}^{T}\right)=0\\
&\bm{U}^{T}\bm{U}=\bm{I}_{r_{1}},\ \bm{V}_{(i)}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i)}=\bm{I}_{r_{2,(i)}},\ \bm{U}^{T}\bm{V}_{i}=0\\
\end{aligned}\right.

(38)

By left multiplying the first equation in (
38
) with
\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}
, we have
\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i)}=0
, which is the first equation in (
24
). By left multiplying the first equation in (
38
) with
\bm{U}^{T}
, we have
\bm{\Lambda}_{(3,(i))}=-\bm{U}^{T}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i)}
. Plugging this into the second equation in (
38
), we have
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}\right]+\bm{U}\left(\bm{\Lambda}_{1}+\bm{\Lambda}_{1}^{T}\right)=0
. We then left multiply both sides again by
\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}
. The second equation in (
24
) follows accordingly. One can also infer (
38
) from (
24
).

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
2

Inspired by
Birnbaum et al. 2013
, in this section, we will use a “spiked population model” to demonstrate the lower bound. We will first use matrix perturbation analysis to estimate the leading order term for the estimation error of global PCs. Then, we verify our results through numerical experiments.

Proof.

To prove theorem
2
, it suffices to find one set of parameters under which the statistical error is indeed
\Omega\left(\frac{1}{\theta}+\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}\right)
. For simplicity, we consider
N=2
and
r_{1}=r_{2}=1
, i.e., each client is driven by one global feature and one local feature. We define a few needed signal vectors
\bm{w}_{1,1},\bm{w}_{1,2},\bm{w}_{2,1},\bm{w}_{2,2}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and a noise vector
\bm{w}_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
as

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{1,1}=\left(\cos\gamma\sin\alpha,\sin\alpha\sin\gamma,\cos\alpha,0\right)^{T}

(39)

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{1,2}=\left(\cos\gamma\cos\alpha,\cos\alpha\sin\gamma,-\sin\alpha,0\right)^{T}

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{2,1}=\left(\cos\gamma\sin\alpha,-\sin\alpha\sin\gamma,\cos\alpha,0\right)^{T}

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{2,2}=\left(\cos\gamma\cos\alpha,-\cos\alpha\sin\gamma,-\sin\alpha,0\right)^{T}

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{3}=\left(0,0,0,1\right)^{T}

Then, we define the population covariance matrix as,

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{1}=2\bm{w}_{1,1}\bm{w}_{1,1}^{T}+\bm{w}_{1,2}\bm{w}_{1,2}^{T}+\varrho\bm{w}_{3}\bm{w}_{3}^{T}

(40)

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{2}=2\bm{w}_{2,1}\bm{w}_{2,1}^{T}+\bm{w}_{2,2}\bm{w}_{2,2}^{T}+\varrho\bm{w}_{3}\bm{w}_{3}^{T}

where
\varrho
is a constant
\varrho<1
. In (
40
),
2\bm{w}_{1,1}\bm{w}_{1,1}^{T}+\bm{w}_{1,2}\bm{w}_{1,2}^{T}
denotes the signal part in
\bm{\Sigma}_{1}
and
\varrho\bm{w}_{3}\bm{w}_{3}^{T}
denotes the noise part. Apparently, the model (
40
) satisfies assumption 4.1 in the main paper. The eigengap
\delta
is
\delta=1-\varrho
.

It is easy to check that if we run directly on the population covariance matrices
\{\bm{\Sigma}_{(1)},\bm{\Sigma}_{(2)}\}
defined in (
40
), the algorithm would recover the optimal global PC as

\bm{u}=\left(0,0,1,0\right)^{T}

and the optimal local PCs as

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{1}=\left(\cos\gamma,\sin\gamma,0,0\right)^{T}

(41)

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{2}=\left(\cos\gamma,-\sin\gamma,0,0\right)^{T}

It is also easy to see that the misalignment parameter
\theta=\sin^{2}\gamma
when
0\leq\gamma\leq\frac{\pi}{4}
.

We further introduce
\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{1}
and
\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{2}
as,

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{1}=\left(-\sin\gamma,\cos\gamma,0,0\right)^{T}

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{2}=\left(\sin\gamma,\cos\gamma,0,0\right)^{T}

Now we consider the sample covariance matrices
\bm{S}_{1}
and
\bm{S}_{2}
. For simplicity, we assume they are only slightly perturbed from the population covariance matrices;
\bm{S}_{1}=\bm{\Sigma}_{1}+\varepsilon\delta\bm{S}_{1}
and
\bm{S}_{2}=\bm{\Sigma}_{2}+\varepsilon\delta\bm{S}_{2}
, where
\varepsilon<<1
is a small number, and
\delta\bm{S}_{1}
and
\delta\bm{S}_{2}
are defined as,

\displaystyle\delta\bm{S}_{1}=\bm{v}_{1}\bm{w}_{3}^{T}+\bm{w}_{3}\bm{v}_{1}^{T}+\bm{v}_{1}\bm{u}^{T}+\bm{u}\bm{v}_{1}^{T}+\bm{v}_{1}\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{2}^{T}+\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{2}\bm{v}_{1}^{T}+\bm{w}_{3}\bm{u}^{T}+\bm{u}\bm{w}_{3}^{T}

(42)

\displaystyle\delta\bm{S}_{2}=\bm{v}_{2}\bm{w}_{3}^{T}+\bm{w}_{3}\bm{v}_{2}^{T}+\bm{v}_{2}\bm{u}^{T}+\bm{u}\bm{v}_{2}^{T}+\bm{v}_{2}\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{1}^{T}+\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{1}\bm{v}_{2}^{T}

i.e., there are some small perturbations in the sample covariance matrix.
\delta\bm{S}_{1}
and
\delta\bm{S}_{2}
model the small differences between the sample covariance and population covariance matrices. It is easy to calculate that,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(1)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(1)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(2)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(2)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)^{2}=7\varepsilon^{2}

We can run on the sample covariance matrices
\bm{S}_{1}
and
\bm{S}_{2}
. The optimal global and local optimal PCs are denoted as
\hat{\bm{u}}
and
(\hat{\bm{v}}_{1},\hat{\bm{v}}_{2})
. Apparently,
\hat{\bm{u}}
and
(\hat{\bm{v}}_{1},\hat{\bm{v}}_{2})
are a function of
\varepsilon
, and as
\varepsilon
becomes zero, the sample covariance becomes the population covariance, and
(\hat{\bm{u}},\hat{\bm{v}}_{1},\hat{\bm{v}}_{2})
become
(\bm{u},\bm{v}_{1},\bm{v}_{2})
.

To estimate
(\hat{\bm{u}},\hat{\bm{v}}_{1},\hat{\bm{v}}_{2})
when
\varepsilon
is nonzero, we can use the KKT conditions to analyze how
(\hat{\bm{u}},\hat{\bm{v}}_{1},\hat{\bm{v}}_{2})
change with respect to
\varepsilon
. Remember that the KKT conditions (
38
) are,

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{1}\hat{\bm{v}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\hat{\bm{v}}_{1}\lambda_{21}+\hat{\bm{v}}_{1}\hat{\bm{v}}_{1}^{T}\bm{S}_{1}\hat{\bm{u}}

(43)

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{2}\hat{\bm{v}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\hat{\bm{v}}_{2}\lambda_{22}+\hat{\bm{v}}_{2}\hat{\bm{v}}_{2}^{T}\bm{S}_{1}\hat{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle\left(\bm{S}_{1}+\bm{S}_{2}\right)\hat{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle=\hat{\bm{u}}\lambda_{1}+\hat{\bm{u}}\hat{\bm{u}}^{T}\bm{S}_{1}\hat{\bm{v}}_{1}+\hat{\bm{u}}\hat{\bm{u}}^{T}\bm{S}_{2}\hat{\bm{v}}_{2}

Since
\bm{\Sigma}_{(1)}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{(2)}
do not have duplicate eigenvalues, from
Greenbaum et al. 2020
,
(\hat{\bm{u}},\hat{\bm{v}}_{1},\hat{\bm{v}}_{2})
and
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{21},\lambda_{22})
are analytic functions of
\varepsilon
when
\varepsilon
is small. We can thus write the Taylor series expansion of
(\hat{\bm{u}},\hat{\bm{v}}_{1},\hat{\bm{v}}_{2})
as,

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{u}}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\bm{u}+\varepsilon\bm{u}^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\bm{u}^{(2)}+\cdots

(44)

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{v}}_{1}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\bm{v}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{v}_{1}^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\bm{v}_{1}^{(2)}+\cdots

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{v}}_{2}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\bm{v}_{2}+\varepsilon\bm{v}_{2}^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\bm{v}_{2}^{(2)}+\cdots

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}^{(0)}+\varepsilon\lambda_{1}^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda_{1}^{(2)}+\cdots

\displaystyle\lambda_{21}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{21}^{(0)}+\varepsilon\lambda_{21}^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda_{21}^{(2)}+\cdots

\displaystyle\lambda_{22}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{22}^{(0)}+\varepsilon\lambda_{22}^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\lambda_{22}^{(2)}+\cdots

where
\bm{u}^{(1)}
is the first-order coefficient and
\bm{u}^{(2)}
is the second-order coefficient for the expansion of
\hat{\bm{u}}(\varepsilon)
. Similar notations are used for other variables.

Then we can take the expansion (
44
) into the KKT conditions (
43
) and match the
O(\varepsilon)
terms on both sides,

\displaystyle\delta\bm{S}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}+\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\bm{v}_{1}\lambda_{21}^{(1)}+\bm{v}_{1}^{(1)}\lambda_{21}^{(0)}

(45)

\displaystyle+\bm{v}_{1}^{(1)}\bm{v}_{1}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\bm{u}+\bm{v}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}^{(1)}{}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\bm{u}+\bm{v}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}^{T}\delta\bm{S}_{1}\bm{u}+\bm{v}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\bm{u}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\delta\bm{S}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}+\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\bm{v}_{2}\lambda_{22}^{(1)}+\bm{v}_{2}^{(1)}\lambda_{22}^{(0)}

\displaystyle+\bm{v}_{2}^{(1)}\bm{v}_{2}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\bm{u}+\bm{v}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}^{(1)}{}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\bm{u}+\bm{v}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}^{T}\delta\bm{S}_{2}\bm{u}+\bm{v}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\bm{u}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\left(\delta\bm{S}_{1}+\delta\bm{S}_{2}\right)\bm{u}+\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{1}+\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\right)\bm{u}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\bm{u}\lambda_{1}^{(1)}+\bm{u}^{(1)}\lambda_{1}^{(0)}

\displaystyle+\bm{u}^{(1)}\bm{u}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}+\bm{u}\bm{u}^{(1)}{}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}+\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\delta\bm{S}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}+\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{1}\bm{v}_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\bm{u}^{(1)}\bm{u}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}+\bm{u}\bm{u}^{(1)}{}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}+\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\delta\bm{S}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}+\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{2}\bm{v}_{2}^{(1)}

To solve equation (
45
), we can expand
\bm{u}^{(1)}
,
\bm{v}_{1}^{(1)}
, and
\bm{v}_{2}^{(1)}
over a basis,

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{00}\bm{u}+\varphi_{01}\bm{v}_{1}+\varphi_{02}\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{2}+\varphi_{03}\bm{w}_{3}

(46)

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{10}\bm{u}+\varphi_{11}\bm{v}_{1}+\varphi_{12}\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{2}+\varphi_{13}\bm{w}_{3}

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{20}\bm{u}+\varphi_{21}\bm{v}^{\perp}{}_{1}+\varphi_{22}\bm{v}_{2}+\varphi_{23}\bm{w}_{3}

Since
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{u}}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\hat{\bm{v}}_{1}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\hat{\bm{v}}_{2}\right\rVert=1
, we know that
\varphi_{00}=\varphi_{11}=\varphi_{22}=0
. Then we can take (
46
) into (
45
), and solve
\varphi
’s as,

\displaystyle\varphi_{01}=-\frac{1}{4}\sin(2\alpha)\cot(\gamma)

(47)

\displaystyle\varphi_{02}=-\frac{1}{2}\sin(\alpha)\cos(\alpha)

\displaystyle\varphi_{03}=-\frac{2\sin(2\alpha)+\cos(2\alpha)+2\varrho-3}{4\left(\varrho^{2}-3\varrho+2\right)}

\displaystyle\varphi_{10}=\frac{1}{4}\sin(2\alpha)\cot(\gamma)

\displaystyle\varphi_{12}=\frac{1}{4}(\cos(2\alpha)+3)

\displaystyle\varphi_{13}=-\frac{\sin(2\alpha)-2\cos(2\alpha)+4\varrho-6}{4\left(\varrho^{2}-3\varrho+2\right)}

\displaystyle\varphi_{20}=\frac{1}{4}\sin(2\alpha)\cot(\gamma)

\displaystyle\varphi_{21}=\frac{1}{4}(\cos(2\alpha)+3)

\displaystyle\varphi_{23}=-\frac{\sin(2\alpha)-2\cos(2\alpha)+4\varrho-6}{4\left(\varrho^{2}-3\varrho+2\right)}

Now, we obtained the closed-form formula for the first-order perturbation of global and local PCs. It is straightforward to calculate that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\hat{\bm{u}}\hat{\bm{u}}^{T}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

(48)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\bm{u}+\varepsilon\bm{u}^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\bm{u}^{(2)}+\cdots\right)\left(\bm{u}+\varepsilon\bm{u}^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\bm{u}^{(2)}+\cdots\right)^{T}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}2\left\lVert\bm{u}^{(1)}\right\rVert^{2}+O\left(\varepsilon^{3}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{64}\left(3\csc^{2}(\gamma)+\frac{\left(4\varrho+2\sqrt{3}-5\right)^{2}}{\left(1-\varrho\right)^{2}\left(2-\varrho\right)^{2}}\right)+O(\varepsilon^{3})

Since we know that
\theta=\sin^{2}(\gamma)
and
\delta=1-\varrho
when
\gamma\leq\frac{\pi}{4}
, we have,

\left\lVert\hat{\bm{u}}\hat{\bm{u}}^{T}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{64}\left(3\frac{1}{\theta}+\frac{\left(2\sqrt{3}-1-4\delta\right)^{2}}{\delta^{2}\left(\delta+1\right)^{2}}\right)+O(\varepsilon^{3})

(49)

When
\varepsilon
is small, the higher order term
O\left(\varepsilon^{3}\right)
can be neglected. Thus the error in (
49
) can be further simplified to
\Omega\left(\varepsilon^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\theta}+\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}\right)\right)
when
\theta
and
\delta
are small. This completes our proof.
∎

We also verify the predicted error (
49
) via numerical simulations. In the simulations, we run on the sample covariance matrices
\bm{S}_{1}
and
\bm{S}_{2}
to obtain the global PC
\hat{\bm{u}}
. Then we use
\hat{\bm{u}}
to calculate the subspace error
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{u}}\hat{\bm{u}}^{T}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\right\rVert
. This is the actual statistical error for the estimates from . We compare it with the predicted values in (
49
) under different parameter values of
\theta
and
\delta
. Results are shown in Figure
11
and
11
.

Figure 10:
The (rescaled) global PC error
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{u}}\hat{\bm{u}}^{T}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}/\varepsilon^{2}
under different eigengap
\delta
.

Figure 11:
The (rescaled) global PC error
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{u}}\hat{\bm{u}}^{T}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}/\varepsilon^{2}
under different misalignment parameter
\theta
.

Figure
11
and
11
demonstrate good matches between the predicted statistical error and the actual statistical error. The two curves vividly show that when
\frac{1}{\theta}
and
\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}
is large, the global subspace error grows linearly with
\frac{1}{\theta}
and
\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}
.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
8

Now we analyze the global convergence of Algorithm
2
. We begin by calculating the derivative of
\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}
. The derivative of
\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}
over
\bm{U}
is:

\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}}\right)\bm{U}

(50)

When
\bm{V}^{T}\bm{U}=0
, this reduces to:

\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}

And the derivative of
\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}
over
\bm{V}
is:

\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}+\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}\bm{V}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}}\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}\bm{V}

(51)

When
\bm{V}^{T}\bm{U}=0
, this reduces to:

\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\bm{P}_{\bm{U}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}

Similarly, the derivative of
\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}
over
\bm{V}
is:

\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=-\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}

(52)

The following lemma shows that the function we introduced is Lipschitz continuous.

Lemma 14
.

When
\left\lVert\bm{U}\right\rVert_{op}
and
\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert_{op}
are upper bounded by 1, the functions
\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}+\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}
are Lipschitz continuous with constant
L
. More formally, for any
\bm{U}_{1},\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{1},\bm{V}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
, such that
\left\lVert\bm{U}_{1}\right\rVert_{op},\left\lVert\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{op},\left\lVert\bm{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{op},\left\lVert\bm{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{op}\leq 1
, we have:

\displaystyle\Big\lVert\Big[\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1}),

(53)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})+\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})\Big]\Big\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq L\sqrt{\left\lVert\bm{U}_{1}-\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{V}_{1}-\bm{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}

where

L=9\sqrt{2}G_{(i),op}

(54)

Proof.

First, we calculate the difference in the gradient of
\bm{U}
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{V}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{T}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{1}-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{V}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{T}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{V}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{T}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{1}-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{V}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{T}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{1}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{V}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{T}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{1}-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{V}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{T}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{V}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{T}-\bm{V}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}G_{(i),op}+\left\lVert\bm{U}_{1}-\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}G_{(i),op}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\bm{V}_{1}-\bm{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}G_{(i),op}+\left\lVert\bm{U}_{1}-\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}G_{(i),op}

where we used the triangle inequality for the Frobenius norm for the first inequality, and Lemma
23
for the second and third inequality. Next, we calculate the difference in the gradient of
\bm{V}
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})+\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\left(\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{2}}-\bm{I}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{2}-\left(\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{1}}-\bm{I}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{2}}\bm{V}_{2}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{2}}\bm{V}_{2}\right\rVert

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{2}}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{2}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V_{2}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{1}}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{1}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{2}}\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{2}}\bm{V}_{2}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{1}}\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{1}}\bm{V}_{1}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 7\left\lVert\bm{V}_{1}-\bm{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}G_{(i),op}+6\left\lVert\bm{U}_{1}-\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}G_{(i),op}

Summing them up, we know:

\displaystyle\Big\lVert\Big[\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1}),

(55)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})+\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})\Big]\Big\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})+\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 9\left\lVert\bm{V}_{1}-\bm{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}G_{(i),op}+7\left\lVert\bm{U}_{1}-\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}G_{(i),op}

\displaystyle\leq 9\sqrt{2}G_{max,op}\sqrt{\left\lVert\bm{U}_{1}-\bm{U}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{V}_{1}-\bm{V}_{2}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}

We thus complete the proof.
∎

Now we introduce some notations:

\square\bm{U}_{\tau}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau}

(56)

It is easy to verify
\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\in\mathcal{T}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}
when
\bm{U}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}=0
:

\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}=0

The Frobenius norm of
\square\bm{U}_{\tau}
is upper bounded by:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

(57)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}

\displaystyle=G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}

where we applied Lemma
23
for the first inequality.

By the client update rule, we know that:

\bm{U}_{(i),\tau+1}=\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau}

(58)

Therefore, after the server takes the average of
\bm{U}_{(i),\tau+1}
and performs a generalized retraction, the following holds:

\bm{U}_{\tau+1}=\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,\tau}

(59)

where
\bm{e}_{1,\tau}
is an error term defined as:

\bm{e}_{1,\tau}=\frac{1}{\eta_{\tau}^{2}}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right)

By definition of a generalized retraction, since
\square\bm{U}_{\tau}
is in the tangent space of
\bm{U}_{\tau}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\leq M_{1}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

where we applied the condition
\eta_{\tau}\leq\frac{M_{3}}{\sqrt{r}G_{max,op}}
thus
\left\lVert\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\leq M_{3}
. Remember that
M_{3}
is a numerical constant in the definition of generalized retraction (Definition
5
).

Similarly, we define:

\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}=\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}

(60)

Also by Lemma
23
, the Frobenius norm of
\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}
is upper bounded by:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

(61)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq G_{(i),op}\sqrt{r}

Now we calculate the update of
\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}
in one communication round. We summarize the result in the following lemma.

Lemma 15
.

If we choose the stepsize
\eta_{\tau}\leq\min\left\{\frac{M_{3}}{2},\frac{\sqrt{M_{3}}}{\sqrt{6+12M_{1}+M_{1}^{2}}}\right\}\frac{1}{G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}}
, the update of
\bm{V}_{(i)}
is given by:

\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}=\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}

where
\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}
is an error term that satisfies:

\left\lVert\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rVert\leq C_{5,0}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert^{2}+C_{5,1}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert^{2}

where
C_{5,0}
and
C_{5,1}
are two constants that only depend on
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
from the generalized retraction Definition
5
.

Proof.

We first calculate the projection:

\displaystyle\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right)\left(\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\eta_{\tau}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\right)+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}

where
\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}
is defined as:

\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}=\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}^{T}+\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}^{T}+\eta_{\tau}\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}^{T}

Its norm is upper bounded by:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}+2\left\lVert\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+2\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{1,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}+2M_{1}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}+2\eta_{\tau}M_{1}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert^{3}_{F}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}M_{1}^{2}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq(1+3M_{1}+\frac{1}{4}M_{1}^{2})\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}

where the final inequality comes from upper bound (
57
) and the choice of stepsize
\eta_{\tau}
:
\eta_{\tau}\leq\frac{1}{G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{6+12M_{1}+M_{1}^{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{2G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}}
.

Similarly, we define
\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}
as:

\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}=\frac{1}{\eta_{\tau}^{2}}\left(\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right)

By definition of a retraction, the norm of
\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}
is upper bounded by:

\left\lVert\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\leq M_{1}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

Then

\displaystyle\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}+\eta_{\tau}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\right)\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\left(\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}\right)

\displaystyle+\eta_{\tau}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\right)\left(\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}\right)+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}

\displaystyle+\eta_{\tau}^{3}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\right)\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}

where we use
\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}
to denote:

\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}=\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}+\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}+\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\right)\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}

Its norm is upper bounded as:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\bm{e}_{2,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\eta_{\tau}\left(\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\right)\left\lVert\bm{e}_{3,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4,0}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+C_{4,1}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

where

C_{4,0}=\frac{3}{2}+3M_{1}+\frac{1}{4}M_{1}^{2}

and

C_{4,1}=\frac{1}{2}+2M_{1}

Thus we know when
\eta_{\tau}\leq\min\{\frac{M_{3}}{2},\sqrt{\frac{M_{3}}{4C_{4,0}}}\}\frac{1}{G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}}
,
\left\lVert\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert_{F}\leq M_{3}

Next we calculate the projection
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{N}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}}}\left(-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{N}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}}}\left(-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)=-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\left(\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}^{T}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}^{T}\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}

We use
\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}
to denote the difference between
\mathcal{GR}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)
and
\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}
, then
its norm is upper bounded by:

\displaystyle\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathcal{GR}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathcal{GR}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}+\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

By property
2
of the generalized retraction in Definition
5
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{GR}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)-\left(\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\left\lVert\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}}}\left(-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+(M_{2}+1)\left\lVert\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{N}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}}}\left(-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\left\lVert-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+(M_{2}+1)\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}^{T}\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=M_{1}\left\lVert\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+(M_{2}+1)\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}^{T}\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2M_{1}\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left(\left\lVert\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\eta_{\tau}^{4}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)+(M_{2}+1)\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}^{T}\bm{e}_{4,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\left(2M_{1}(C_{4,0}+C_{4,1})C_{4,0}+2M_{1}+M_{2}C_{4,0}\right)

\displaystyle+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\left(2M_{1}(C_{4,0}+C_{4,1})C_{4,1}+M_{2}C_{4,1}\right)

For the second part:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{U}_{\tau+1}\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert e_{3,(n),\tau}-e_{4,(n),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert e_{3,(n),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert e_{4,(n),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

Therefore, the norm of
\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}
is upper bounded as:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\left(2M_{1}(C_{4,0}+C_{4,1})C_{4,0}+2M_{1}+M_{2}C_{4,0}+C_{4,0}\right)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\left(2M_{1}(C_{4,0}+C_{4,1})C_{4,1}+M_{2}C_{4,1}+M_{1}+C_{4,1}\right)

This completes our proof, with

C_{5,0}=\frac{1}{8}\left(12\left(M_{2}+1\right)+M_{1}\left(24M_{2}+M_{1}\left(M_{1}\left(M_{1}\left(M_{1}+32\right)+254\right)+2\left(M_{2}+109\right)\right)+88\right)\right)

and

C_{5,1}=M_{1}^{4}+\frac{81M_{1}^{3}}{4}+13M_{1}^{2}+\left(2M_{2}+5\right)M_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(M_{2}+1\right)

∎

The following lemma shows the sufficient decrease property:

Lemma 16
.

(Formal version of Lemma
9
) When we choose the stepsize
\eta_{\tau}\leq\frac{1}{G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}}\min\left\{\frac{M_{3}}{2},\frac{\sqrt{M_{3}}}{\sqrt{6+12M_{1}+M_{1}^{2}}}\right\}
, and
\bm{U}_{\tau}
and
\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}
satisfy the orthogonality condition
\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}=0
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}),\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle

(62)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle\nabla_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau})+\nabla_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}),\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-\eta_{\tau}N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-\eta_{\tau}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left(C_{6,0}N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+C_{6,1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

where
C_{6,0}
and
C_{6,1}
are constants dependent only on
M_{1}
,
M_{2}
,
r
, and
G_{max,op}
:

C_{6,0}=G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}(M_{1}+C_{5,0})

and

C_{6,1}=G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}C_{5,1}

Proof.

We firstly calculate the sufficient decrease of
\bm{U}
:

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}),\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau},\frac{\eta_{\tau}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau},\frac{\eta_{\tau}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau},\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau},\frac{\eta_{\tau}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau},\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-\eta_{\tau}N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq-\eta_{\tau}N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+M_{1}\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}G_{(i),op}\sqrt{r}

Next, we calculate:

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau})+\nabla_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}),\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau},\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau},\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle+\left\langle-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau},\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau},\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau},\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle+\left\langle-\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau},\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq-\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}G_{(i),op}\sqrt{r}\left(C_{5,0}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+C_{5,1}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert^{2}\right)

Adding them, we have:

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}),\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\nabla_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),1}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau})+\nabla_{\bm{V}_{(i)}}\mathcal{L}_{(i),2}(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}),\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-\eta_{\tau}N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left(NC_{6,0}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+C_{6,1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

where the constants are:

C_{6,0}=G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}(M_{1}+C_{5,0})

and

C_{6,1}=G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}C_{5,1}

∎

Finally, we come to the proof of Theorem
8
.

Proof.

We choose constant a stepsize
\eta_{\tau}=\eta_{1}
small enough:

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{c}=

\displaystyle\min\Big\{\frac{1}{2C_{6,0}+L\left(\left(\left(1+\frac{M_{2}}{2}\right)^{2}+2\left(1+\frac{C_{5,0}}{2}\right)^{2}\right)\right)},\frac{1}{2C_{6,1}+2L\left(1+\frac{C_{5,1}}{2}\right)^{2}},

(63)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}}\frac{M_{3}}{2},\frac{1}{G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}}\frac{\sqrt{M_{3}}}{\sqrt{6+12M_{1}+M_{1}^{2}}}\Big\}

Obviously,
\eta_{1}
satisfies the requirement in Lemma
15
and
16
.

By the property of Lipschitz continuity, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{(i)}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau+1},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}\right)\leq\mathcal{L}_{(i)}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i)}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right),\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i)}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right),\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{L}{2}\left(\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

where
L
is defined in (
54
). Since
\bm{U}_{\tau+1}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}=0
and
\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}=0
, we know that

\mathcal{L}_{(i)}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau+1},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}\right)=-f_{i}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau+1},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}\right)

and that

\mathcal{L}_{(i)}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right)=-f_{i}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right)

Then, summing up both sides for
n
from 1 to
N
, we have:

\displaystyle-f\left(\bm{U}_{\tau+1},\{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}\}\right)\leq-f\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\}\right)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i)}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right),\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}_{(i)}\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right),\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{L}{2}\left(\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

From equation (
59
), we know

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{U}_{\tau+1}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{1,\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}M_{1}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}(1+\frac{M_{1}}{2})\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

Similarly, from Lemma
15
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}-\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\eta_{\tau}\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\bm{U}_{\tau}\square\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{e}_{5,(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\left(1+C_{5,0}\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\right)+\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\left(1+C_{5,1}\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\left(1+\frac{1}{2}C_{5,1}\right)+\eta_{\tau}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}\left(1+\frac{1}{2}C_{5,0}\right)

Combining the two inequalities and Lemma
16
, we have:

\displaystyle-f\left(\bm{U}_{\tau+1},\{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau+1}\}\right)

(64)

\displaystyle\leq-f\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\}\right)-\eta_{\tau}\left(N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\eta_{\tau}^{2}NC_{6,0}\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}C_{6,1}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle+\eta_{\tau}^{2}\frac{L}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\left(\left(1+\frac{M_{2}}{2}\right)^{2}+2\left(1+\frac{C_{5,0}}{2}\right)^{2}\right)\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+2\left(1+\frac{C_{5,1}}{2}\right)^{2}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-f\left(\bm{U}_{\tau},\{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\}\right)-\frac{\eta_{\tau}}{2}\left(N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

Summing up both sides for
\tau
from
1
to
R
and rearranging terms, we have:

\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}\sum_{\tau=1}^{R}\left(N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)\leq-f(\bm{U}_{1},\{\bm{V}_{(i),1}\})+f(\bm{U}_{R+1},\{\bm{V}_{(i),R+1}\})

As a result,

\min_{\tau\in\{1,\cdots,N\}}\sum_{\tau=1}^{R}\left(N\left\lVert\square\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\square\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)\leq\frac{2\left(f(\bm{U}_{R+1},\{\bm{V}_{(i),R+1}\})-f(\bm{U}_{1},\{\bm{V}_{(i),1}\})\right)}{R\eta_{1}}

This completes the proof of Theorem
8
. Notice that
C_{6,0}
,
C_{6,1}
, and
L
are of the order
G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}
, thus the requirement on
\eta_{c}
in equation (
63
) becomes:

\eta_{c}=C_{\eta}\frac{1}{G_{max,op}\sqrt{r}}

where
C_{\eta}
is a constant that only depends on
M_{1}
,
M_{2}
, and
M_{3}
from the generalized retraction Definition
5
.
∎

Appendix E
Proof for local linear convergence

In this section, we will show the full proof of Theorem
10
. A formal theorem is stated below.

Theorem 17
.

(Formal version of theorem
10
) Under assumptions
4.2
,
6.1
, and
6.2
, if the difference between the population and sample covariance is small
\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}\leq\min\{\frac{\sqrt{2}-1}{4}\mu\theta^{3/2},\frac{\mu^{2}\theta^{2}}{128^{2}\times 2G_{max,op}}\}
and
\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert\leq G_{max,op}
, when we initialize close to the global optimum
\phi_{0}\leq\phi_{\tau}\leq\frac{\mu^{3}\theta^{3}}{411041792G_{max,op}^{2}}
, and choose a constant stepsize
\eta_{t}=\eta=O\left(\frac{1}{G_{op,max}\sqrt{r}}\right)
, then Algorithm
2
with choice 1 will converge into the global optimum:

f(\hat{\bm{U}},\{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}\})-f(\bm{U}_{R},\{\bm{V}_{(i),R}\})=O\left(\left(1-\eta\frac{\mu\theta}{32}\right)^{R}\right)

where
\{\hat{\bm{U}},\{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}\}\}
is a set of optimal solutions to problem (
7
).

Furthermore, we can recover the exact global optimal solutions:

\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{R}}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}_{g}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),R}}-\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}=O\left(\left(1-\eta\frac{\mu\theta}{32}\right)^{R}\right)

We will start by introducing needed notations, then proceed to establish some lemmas that characterize the local geometry of the optimization objective, then prove Theorem
10
at the end.

At communication round
\tau
, remember that we use
\bm{U}_{\tau}
and
\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}
to denote the updated variables. We use
\left(\hat{\bm{U}},\{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}\}\right)
to denote one set of optimal solutions to (
7
). For simplicity, we use
\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}
to denote the projection
\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}=\hat{\bm{U}}\hat{\bm{U}}^{T}
and
\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}
to denote the projection
\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}=\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}^{T}
.

Since each covariance matrix
\bm{S}_{(i)}
is symmetric positive semidefinite, we can find matrix
\bm{F}_{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that
\bm{F}_{(i)}\bm{F}_{(i)}^{T}=\bm{S}_{(i)}
by Cholesky factorization. Furthermore, we can define
\bm{F}_{(i),g}
,
\bm{F}_{(i),l}
, and
\bm{R}_{(i)}
as,

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{(i),g}

\displaystyle=\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}\bm{F}_{(i)}

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle=\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\bm{F}_{(i)}

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle=\bm{F}_{(i),g}+\bm{F}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{(i)}

\displaystyle=\bm{F}_{(i)}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}\bm{F}_{(i)}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\bm{F}_{(i)}

Apparently,
\bm{F}_{(i),g}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}=\bm{F}_{(i),l}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}=0
.

Next we will introduce a set of optimal solutions
\left(\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau},\{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}\}\right)
that is close to the current updates
(\bm{U}_{\tau},\{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\}
. The variables
\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}
and
\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}
’s are defined as

\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}=\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}\bm{U}_{\tau}\left((\bm{U}_{\tau})^{T}\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}\bm{U}_{\tau}\right)^{-1/2}

(66)

and

\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}=\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\left(\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right)^{-1/2}

(67)

for each
n=1,...N
.

It’s easy to verify that

\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}(\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau})^{T}=\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}

and that

\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}(\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau})^{T}=\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}

Notice that
\{\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau},\{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}\}\}

is one set of global optimal solutions that is dependent on the iteration index
\tau
. The
\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}
and
\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}
’s are dependent on the communication round
\tau
. We use
\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}
to denote the difference between
\bm{U}_{\tau}
and
\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}
:

\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}=\bm{U}_{\tau}-\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}

(68)

and similarly:

\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}=\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}-\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}

(69)

Since
\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}
and
\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}
’s are optimal, we can simplify the KKT conditions in (
24
) as

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{(i)}\bm{F}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}=0,\quad\forall i\in[N]

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bm{R}_{(i)}\bm{F}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}=0

(70)

We can replace
\bm{F}_{(i)}
by
\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}
in (
E
) since
\bm{F}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}=\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}
and
\bm{F}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}=\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}
.

We will first show some properties of the introduced variables.

Lemma 18
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
10
, there exists constants
\hat{\theta}=\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{2}}
,
\hat{\mu}=\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{2}}
, such that the following holds,

1.

\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{2G_{max,op}}
.

2.

The smallest nonzero eigenvalue of
\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}
is lower bounded by
\hat{\mu}
.

3.

\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right\rVert\leq 1-\hat{\theta}
.

4.

\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\hat{\mu}\hat{\theta}}{64\sqrt{2G_{max,op}}}=\frac{\mu\theta}{128\sqrt{2G_{max,op}}}

We will use the
\hat{\theta}
and
\hat{\mu}
notations in the remaining parts of the section.

Proof.

We will prove the claims one by one.

From (
34
), we know,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{N\delta^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

where we replace
\delta
by
\mu
since
\left(\bm{I}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}=0
.

Therefore, the difference between
\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}
is upper bounded by,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8G_{max,op}}{\mu}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(j)}-\bm{S}_{(j)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}+\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu^{2}\theta^{2}}{128^{2}\times 2G_{max,op}}

From Weyl’s theorem, we know,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2G_{max,op}

This proves Claim
1
.

Also by Weyl’s theorem, we know,

\displaystyle\lambda_{2r}\left(\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{2r}\left(\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right)-\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\geq\mu-\frac{\mu^{2}\theta^{2}}{32768G_{max,op}}

\displaystyle\geq\mu\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}

This proves Claim
2
.

Next, we consider the results from Theorem
1
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{U}}}-\bm{\Pi}_{g}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{8}{\theta\mu^{2}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

Therefore, an upper bound for
\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}
is

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{P}_{\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i)}}-\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\theta}\mu}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\theta\frac{2-\sqrt{2}}{2}

where we applied the condition that
\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}\leq\mu\theta^{1.5}\frac{\sqrt{2}-1}{4}
in the last inequality.

As a result, we have,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rVert+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 1-\theta+\theta\left(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{2}}

This proves Claim
3
.

Then we analyze the norm of
\bm{R}_{(i)}\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\left(\bm{I}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\left(\bm{I}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\left(\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right)\left(\bm{I}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{g}+\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{S}_{(i)}-\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}\left(1+4\frac{G_{max,op}}{\delta}\right)\frac{\mu\theta}{128\sqrt{2G_{max,op}}}

where the last inequality comes from the fact that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\bm{\Sigma}_{(i)}-\bm{S}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\left(1+\frac{8G_{max,op}}{\mu}\right)^{2}}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}\theta^{2}}{32768G_{max,op}}\right)^{2}
.

∎

As discussed in Section
6.1.1
, different
\bm{U}
and
\bm{V}_{(i)}
’s may have the same objective value, as long as they span the same column space. We introduce a variable
\zeta
to denote the subspace distance between the estimate and ground truth:

\zeta_{(i),\tau}=r-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}},\bm{\Pi}_{(i)}\right\rangle

(71)

for each
i=1,...N
, and,

\zeta_{(0),\tau}=r-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}},\bm{\Pi}_{g}\right\rangle

(72)

We use
\zeta_{\tau}
to denote:

\zeta_{\tau}=\zeta_{(0),\tau}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\zeta_{(i),\tau}

(73)

The
\zeta_{(0),\tau}
and
\zeta_{(i),\tau}
’s defined represent how far away the iterates are from the ground truth, measured by subspace distance.

We can also define,

\widetilde{\zeta}_{(i),\tau}=2r-\left\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}+\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}},\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right\rangle

(74)

for each
i=1,...N
. We use
\widetilde{\zeta}_{\tau}
to denote:

\widetilde{\zeta}_{\tau}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\zeta}_{(i),\tau}

(75)

From Lemma
13
, since
\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}
’s are
\hat{\theta}
-misaligned, there exists a relation between
\zeta_{\tau}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}_{\tau}
:

\frac{\hat{\theta}}{2}N\zeta_{\tau}\leq\widetilde{\zeta}_{\tau}\leq N\zeta_{\tau}

(76)

For simplicity, we also define the optimality gap
\phi_{\tau}
as,

\displaystyle\phi_{\tau}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\text{Tr}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)+\text{Tr}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)-\text{Tr}\left(\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)-\text{Tr}\left(\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)\right)

(77)

We then use the optimality gap
\phi_{\tau}
to upper bound the norm of
\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}
and
\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}
.

Lemma 19
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
10
, we have,

\displaystyle\phi_{\tau}\geq\frac{\hat{\theta}\hat{\mu}}{16}\left(N\left\lVert\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

(78)

Proof.

By definition of the optimality gap
\phi_{\tau}
, we have,

\displaystyle 2\phi_{\tau}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\text{Tr}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)+\text{Tr}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)-\text{Tr}\left(\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)-\text{Tr}\left(\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\bm{S}_{(i)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\left\lVert\bm{F}_{(i)}-\left(\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}+\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-\left\lVert\bm{F}_{(i)}-\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\bm{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}+\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\underbrace{2\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)},\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rangle}_{\texttt{Term I}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}_{\texttt{Term II}}

The above can be further simplified. For
Term I
, we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)},\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(-\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}-\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right)

where we have applied the KKT conditions (
E
) that
\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}=0
and
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}^{T}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}=0
in the third equality.

For term
Term II
, we can also derive

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right)-\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}\right)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right)

where we used the condition
\hat{\bm{U}}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}=\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}=0
in the last equality.

Combining these, we have,

\displaystyle 2\phi_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big(\left\lVert\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}-\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right)

\displaystyle-2\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right)\Big)

(79)

From Lemma
18
, we know that
\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}\succeq\hat{\mu}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)
, thus

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\text{Tr}\left(\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}^{T}\right)\geq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\text{Tr}\left(\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}+\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\right)\right)\hat{\mu}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\hat{\mu}\hat{\theta}}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(r-\text{Tr}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{g}\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}\right)+r-\text{Tr}\left(\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\hat{\mu}\hat{\theta}}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\left\lVert\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have,

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)}\right)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}\right)+\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert^{2}

and

\displaystyle 2\text{Tr}\left(\bm{R}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}^{T}+\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert+2\left\lVert\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert

Since
\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\hat{\mu}\hat{\theta}}{64\sqrt{2G_{max,op}}}
and
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{2G_{max,op}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert^{2}+2\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 3\left\lVert\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rVert\left\lVert\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\hat{\mu}\hat{\theta}}{8}

Thus we have,

\displaystyle 2\phi_{\tau}\geq\frac{\hat{\mu}\hat{\theta}}{8}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\left\lVert\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

This completes our proof.
∎

Next we will provide a lemma that characterizes the landscape of the objective.

Lemma 20
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
10
, we have,

\displaystyle-\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{U}_{\tau},\Delta\bm{U}_{\tau}\right\rangle-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau},\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\phi_{\tau}

Proof.

We first consider the inner product term,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{S}_{(i)}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau},\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{F}_{(i)},\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\bm{F}_{(i)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)},\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rangle}_{\texttt{Term III}}+\underbrace{\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)},\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rangle}_{\texttt{Term IV}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)},\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\bm{R}_{(i)}\right\rangle}_{\texttt{Term V}}+\underbrace{\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\bm{R}_{(i)},\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rangle}_{\texttt{Term VI}}

We will analyze each term separately. For
Term III
, we know that
\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}=\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}-\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}-\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)},\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)},\left(\bm{P}_{\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}}-\hat{\bm{\Pi}}_{(i)}-\hat{\bm{V}}_{(i),\tau}\Delta\bm{V}_{(i),\tau}^{T}\right)\hat{\bm{F}}_{(i)}\right\rangle
